| ----- | ----------- | ----------------------------- | ---------------------- | ------------------------ |
| Light | `#f3eee5`   | `#ece6db`, 1.07:1             | `#e7e2da`, 1.12:1      | `#e2ddd4`, 1.17:1        |
| Dark  | `#24211d`   | `#2d2924`, 1.11:1             | `#302c28`, 1.16:1      | `#35322d`, 1.26:1        |

The ratios are against `--sidebar`. In both themes the hover and pressed fills differ from `--sidebar` more than the selected fill does. The user kept it so (SQ11, U17): the selected fill stays `bg-sidebar-accent`, the color of a selected row before phase 5 too (`data-active:bg-sidebar-accent`, `sidebar.tsx:497`, `:701`). The paper palette's selected-item token, `--paper-thumbnail-selected` (light `#f1eadc`, `tokens.css:52`; dark `#3a342c`, `:147`, `:223`; the project list's highlighted row, `project-item.tsx:74`), would not change that in the light theme: it has 1.04:1 against `--sidebar`, less than the selected fill's 1.07:1 and the hover fill's 1.12:1. In the dark theme it has 1.30:1, above the hover fill's 1.16:1, so only there would it differ from `--sidebar` more than the hover fill; it is not used.

Class sets. These are the exact class tokens the rows take; the sections below say which element takes which set ([§11.5](#115-where-the-classes-live-and-mixed-versions) lists them per file):

| Set                     | Since                      | Taken by                                                                                                                                              | Tokens                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

| Selected fill           | version 8                  | row wrappers of selected rows ([§11.1](#111-row-fill))                                                                                                | `rounded-[4px] bg-sidebar-accent`                                                                                                                                                                           |

| No-fill button          | version 8                  | row buttons ([§11.1](#111-row-fill))                                                                                                                  | `bg-transparent hover:bg-transparent active:bg-transparent data-active:bg-transparent focus-visible:ring-0`                                                                                                 |
| Unselected mark backing | version 9, rewritten (D27) | mark backings ([§11.2](#112-mark-backing))                                                                                                            | `bg-sidebar group-hover/tree-row:bg-[linear-gradient(var(--paper-ghost-hover),var(--paper-ghost-hover))] group-active/tree-row:bg-[linear-gradient(var(--paper-ghost-pressed),var(--paper-ghost-pressed))]` |
| Selected mark backing   | version 8                  | mark backings of selected rows                                                                                                                        | `bg-sidebar-accent`                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Keyboard reveal on _G_  | version 8 (U14)            | reveals ([§11.7](#117-reveals-on-keyboard-focus-only))                                                                                                | each `group-focus-within/G:` prefix of the element replaced by `group-has-[:focus-visible]/G:`, the rest of each class unchanged                                                                            |

| Action outline          | version 9, new (D29)       | the plus, Remove, Retry, Stop, Sign in                                                                                                                | `focus-visible:outline-solid focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-[var(--sidebar-ring)]`                                                                                                           |
| Stop ring off           | version 9, new (D29)       | Stop                                                                                                                                                  | `focus-visible:ring-0`                                                                                                                                                                                      |

The sets marked "version 8" are those phase 5 built at `e63bbccb`. Version 8's tokens for the rewritten sets are recorded in [Alternatives considered](#alternatives-considered) and in the history of the files at `e63bbccb`.

| Outline             | _S_                               | Focused row control                                                                                                  |
| ------------------- | --------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Treeitem outline    | `[role=treeitem]`                 | a Thread, `FileTree` or repository row's `role="treeitem"` element                                                   |
| Textbox outline     | `[role=textbox]`                  | a Thread's rename field, the draft's name field                                                                      |
| Sub-button outline  | `[data-sidebar=menu-sub-button]`  | a terminal row's button, whose `data-slot` is the context menu trigger's ([§11.3](#113-keyboard-focus-and-clipping)) |
| Input outline       | `[data-slot=input]`               | a terminal's rename field (`packages/desktop/src/components/input.tsx:9`)                                            |
| Menu-button outline | `[data-slot=sidebar-menu-button]` | a Screens or MCP row's button                                                                                        |

How the classes reach a window: the Terms entry "Renderer sheet, bundle sheet" describes the sheets, their order and why a class needs a sheet that scans its source ([§11.5](#115-where-the-classes-live-and-mixed-versions), "Mixed versions", applies it).

#### 11.1 Row fill

The row wrapper draws the fill; the row's button draws none (D19). Two reasons:

- In the Files column trees the mark is the button's sibling ([§10.3](#103-pinned-trailing-marks)), so only the wrapper covers both, and U13 runs the fill under the mark.
- On rows without a mark, controls are drawn over the button as its siblings: a Thread row's plus (`RowAddAction` beside the button, `thread-branches.tsx:201-208`; the plus is `absolute` and turns `pointer-events-auto` on row hover, `row-add-action.tsx:69-77`), a terminal row's Stop (`terminal-rows.tsx:137-149`), and a pane row's Remove and Retry ([§11.6](#116-pane-rows-and-the-draft-row)). While the pointer is over one of them, the button is not `:hover`, so a fill on the button would drop under the pointer. The wrapper contains them, so its `:hover` holds. The elapsed badge is `pointer-events-none` (`sidebar.tsx:611`) and does not take the pointer.

| State          | Unselected row                                                        | Selected row                     |
| -------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------- |
| Rest           | none                                                                  | `bg-sidebar-accent`              |
| Hover          | `hover:bg-[var(--paper-ghost-hover)]`                                 | `bg-sidebar-accent`              |
| Pressed        | `active:bg-[var(--paper-ghost-pressed)]`                              | `bg-sidebar-accent`              |
| Keyboard focus | the ring of [§11.3](#113-keyboard-focus-and-clipping), no fill change | the ring, on `bg-sidebar-accent` |

- Every row wrapper also takes `rounded-[4px]`. That is the radius the buttons have today: `rounded-[4px]` on `FileTree` and repository rows (`file-tree.tsx:248`, `changes-tree.tsx:102`) and `rounded-md` on Thread rows (`thread-branches.tsx:171`), which is `calc(var(--radius) - 2px)` = 4 px (`packages/desktop/src/styles/tailwind.css:65`, `packages/desktop/src/styles/tokens.css:83`).
- Version 9 (D27). The hover and pressed fills are translucent: the ghost tokens are `--sidebar-foreground` at 5.9% and 8.6% alpha ([§11](#11-tree-row-states), the token table). Each row sits on `--sidebar` and no row wrapper lies inside another's fill (nested rows sit outside their parent's wrapper, [§11.3](#113-keyboard-focus-and-clipping)), so the ghost composites once, over `--sidebar`, giving the colors of [§11](#11-tree-row-states)'s second table. The mark backing of [§11.2](#112-mark-backing) paints that same composite as an opaque layer, so over the name it has exactly the color of the row around it. Version 8 used opaque `color-mix` fills for the same reason; the opaque backing keeps that property with translucent fills.
- Pressed replaces hover rather than adding to it: `hover:` and `active:` both set `background-color` with the same specificity, and Tailwind 4.3.3 emits the `active:` rule after the `hover:` rule (checked on the renderer sheet compiled in memory from `app/desktop/doric-renderer/src/styles.css` with these tokens added, 2026-11-11), so a pressed row shows `--paper-ghost-pressed` once over `--sidebar`.
- A selected row keeps `bg-sidebar-accent` under the pointer: the selected set has no `hover:` or `active:` token, as in version 8. No ghost layers over it, so a selected row does not change while hovered or pressed, and its mark backing (`bg-sidebar-accent`) matches it in every state.
- Hover and pressed are the wrapper's own `:hover` and `:active`, which also match while the pointer is over, or presses, any element inside the wrapper: the plus, the elapsed badge, the mark, a terminal row's Stop button. A press on the plus or Stop shows the row's pressed fill (SR18); the action's own hover square then lies over it ([§11.4](#114-terminal-rows-and-row-actions)).
- What counts as selected does not change: a Thread row whose Thread is `selectedThreadId` (`thread-branches.tsx:102`); a `FileTree` file row whose path is `selectedPath` (`file-tree.tsx:237`; directory rows are never selected); the repository row is never selected (`changes-tree.tsx:103`). New in phase 5: a terminal row is selected while its terminal's tab is its Thread's selected tab and the route names that Thread ([§11.4](#114-terminal-rows-and-row-actions)); pane rows and the draft row have no selection ([§11.6](#116-pane-rows-and-the-draft-row)).
- The row button appends `bg-transparent hover:bg-transparent active:bg-transparent data-active:bg-transparent focus-visible:ring-0`. `cn` (the `cn` package 0.3.0, `packages/desktop/package.json:138`, a tailwind-merge replacement) drops the defaults these conflict with: `hover:bg-sidebar-accent`, `active:bg-sidebar-accent`, `data-active:bg-sidebar-accent` and `focus-visible:ring-2` (checked by running `cn` on both lists on 2026-11-11). The button keeps its size, padding, radius, `isActive` (so `data-active` and, in `FileTree`, `aria-selected` stay) and text classes.
- Text colors: the rows stop calling `rowInteraction`, so a button's hover and press text classes are its defaults (`text-sidebar-accent-foreground`, `sidebar.tsx:497`, `:701`) instead of `text-sidebar-foreground`. The two tokens are equal in both themes (`tokens.css:32`, `:36`; `:127`, `:131`), so no text changes color on screen. The mark keeps its `peer-hover/menu-button` and `peer-data-active/menu-button` text classes ([§10.3](#103-pinned-trailing-marks), T-4.1).
- `TreeGuides` keeps drawing its 1 px lines over the row (`absolute inset-0 z-10`, `pointer-events-none`, `tree-guides.tsx:33-38`), now over the wrapper's fill as before over the button's.
- A Thread row's running state does not change: the name's `shimmer`, the elapsed badge, and the plus replacing the badge on hover or keyboard focus (`bundles/desktop/layout/src/client/components/molecules/row-add-action.tsx:52-77`, [§4](#4-thread-rows), [§11.7](#117-reveals-on-keyboard-focus-only)).
- In the Files column trees the wrapper is as wide as the tree ([§10.2](#102-rows)), so the fill runs under the mark and, while the tree is scrolled, across the whole visible row.

#### 11.2 Mark backing

The mark backing (`file-tree.tsx:337`, `changes-tree.tsx:159`) drops `bg-sidebar/90` and takes:

- on an unselected row: `bg-sidebar group-hover/tree-row:bg-[linear-gradient(var(--paper-ghost-hover),var(--paper-ghost-hover))] group-active/tree-row:bg-[linear-gradient(var(--paper-ghost-pressed),var(--paper-ghost-pressed))]` (version 9, D27);
- on a selected row: `bg-sidebar-accent`.

Version 9. `bg-sidebar` sets the backing's `background-color` in every unselected state. Under hover and press, one more class adds a `background-image`: a gradient from the ghost token to itself, a flat translucent layer. A background image paints over the background color of the same element, so the backing is opaque and its color is the ghost composited over `--sidebar`, which is what the row around it shows ([§11.1](#111-row-fill)). Rules the classes depend on, each checked by compiling them with Tailwind 4.3.3 on 2026-11-11:

- The gradient sets the image, not the color. `group-hover/tree-row:bg-[linear-gradient(…)]` compiles to `background-image: linear-gradient(var(--paper-ghost-hover),var(--paper-ghost-hover))` (Tailwind infers an image from the `linear-gradient(` value), and `bg-sidebar` to `background-color: var(--color-sidebar)`. The two set different properties, so `bg-sidebar`'s color holds under the layer, and no other background property is reset.
- The layer applies in its state. The hover class compiles to `.…:is(:where(.group\/tree-row):hover *)` inside `@media (hover: hover)`, and the pressed class to `.…:is(:where(.group\/tree-row):active *)`: one class and one pseudo-class each. No other rule of the backing sets `background-image`, so nothing competes with them but each other.
- Pressed wins over hover. The two rules set `background-image` with the same specificity and the `group-active` rule comes after the `group-hover` rule in the compiled renderer sheet, so a pressed row's backing shows the pressed layer alone, as the row does.
- A selected row's backing takes `bg-sidebar-accent` and no image class, so it has no layer in any state.
- `cn` keeps all three. They carry different variants, so `cn` drops none (`cn('bg-sidebar', '…')` run on 2026-11-11 returns the three tokens), and no other class of the backing element (`file-tree.tsx:373-374` and `changes-tree.tsx:185-187` at `e63bbccb`: `flex h-5 min-w-5 items-center justify-center rounded-md px-1 text-xs tabular-nums pointer-events-none select-none`) sets a background.

The selected backing has no hover or pressed token, like the selected row ([§11.1](#111-row-fill)), so the two match in every state.

`group/tree-row` is the row wrapper, so `group-hover/tree-row` and `group-active/tree-row` hold exactly when the wrapper's `hover:` and `active:` do, and the mark backing changes with the row. It is opaque: a name scrolled under a pinned mark no longer shows through it. The mark's `span` (`sticky right-1 shrink-0 pr-1` and its text classes, `file-tree.tsx:335`, `changes-tree.tsx:157`) is unchanged; its 4 px right padding stays transparent, as today. The mark backing keeps `pointer-events-none`, which its `Badge` inherits, so the badge's own `hover:bg-muted` (`packages/desktop/src/components/badge.tsx:19`) never shows.

#### 11.3 Keyboard focus and clipping

- Version 9 (D28): the row outline is the "Outline on _S_" set of [§11](#11-tree-row-states), `has-[S:focus-visible]:outline-2 has-[S:focus-visible]:-outline-offset-2 has-[S:focus-visible]:outline-[var(--sidebar-ring)]`, on the same wrappers, with the same selectors _S_ and in the same color as version 8's `ring-2 ring-inset ring-sidebar-ring`. Its form is the tab strip's wrapper outline (`packages/desktop/src/panels/tab-strip.tsx:27`, `[&:has(:focus-visible)]:outline-2 [&:has(:focus-visible)]:-outline-offset-1`) with a −2 px offset; its color is `--sidebar-ring` by SQ10 (U17), not the Dashboard's `--paper-focus` ([§11](#11-tree-row-states) lists what is copied and what is new). The rest of this section calls it the row outline.
- The Thread, `FileTree` and repository row wrappers take `has-[[role=treeitem]:focus-visible]:outline-2 has-[[role=treeitem]:focus-visible]:-outline-offset-2 has-[[role=treeitem]:focus-visible]:outline-[var(--sidebar-ring)]`. In each, the `role="treeitem"` element is the row's button (`thread-branches.tsx:155-157`, `file-tree.tsx:253-255`, `changes-tree.tsx:107-109`), and the wrapper holds only its own row: nested rows sit outside it (`thread-branches.tsx:211-234`, `changes-tree.tsx:169-173`; `FileTree` draws one flat line per row).
- A terminal row's `SidebarMenuSubItem` takes the same three classes with `[data-sidebar=menu-sub-button]` in place of `[role=treeitem]`. Its button is a `<button>` with no role (`terminal-rows.tsx:95`), passed as the `render` of `ContextMenuTrigger` (`terminal-rows.tsx:87-101`). That trigger passes `data-slot="context-menu-trigger"` to the element it renders (`packages/desktop/src/components/context-menu.tsx:28`), and base-ui 1.8.0 merges passed props over a component's state attributes (`node_modules/@base-ui/react/internals/useRenderElement.js:65`, `mergeObjects(stateProps, resolvedProps)`). So the rendered button carries `data-slot="context-menu-trigger"`, `data-sidebar="menu-sub-button"` and `data-size="sm"`: `SidebarMenuSubButton`'s `data-slot` (`sidebar.tsx:709`) is replaced and its `data-sidebar` (`sidebar.tsx:710`) is kept, since the trigger passes none. An outline keyed on `data-slot=sidebar-menu-sub-button` would match nothing in the app. The story stand-in has no context menu (`bundles/desktop/layout/stories/support/terminal-rows.tsx:34-53`), so its button keeps both attributes; `[data-sidebar=menu-sub-button]` matches the button in the app and in the stories. The Thread and repository buttons are also context menu triggers (`thread-branches.tsx:137`, `changes-tree.tsx:145`); their row outline is keyed on `role`, which the trigger does not pass, and the Screens and MCP buttons are not wrapped in a trigger (`screen/src/client/elements.tsx:162-176`, `mcp/src/client/elements.tsx:146-159`), so they keep `data-slot="sidebar-menu-button"`.
- The ring shows for keyboard focus (`:focus-visible`) on the row's button, and on the row's rename field (D25, below). Focus on a row action draws that action's own focus mark and not the row's: the plus, Remove and Retry (`SidebarMenuAction`, whose default is `focus-visible:ring-2`, `sidebar.tsx:586`), Stop (`Button`) and Sign in. From version 9 that mark is the action outline, 2 px of `--sidebar-ring` outside the action, over the action's own ring where it keeps one ([§11.4](#114-terminal-rows-and-row-actions), D29). The plus shows while the row is hovered or something in it matches `:focus-visible` ([§11.7](#117-reveals-on-keyboard-focus-only)).
- Rename fields (D25). A Thread or terminal row being renamed draws the row outline while its name field holds focus, as the draft row does ([§11.6](#116-pane-rows-and-the-draft-row)).
  - Thread row: the field is the `EditableName` inside the row's button (`thread-branches.tsx:186-199`), a `role="textbox"` `contentEditable` span while editing (`editable-name.tsx:135-140`). The row wrapper also takes the textbox outline of [§11](#11-tree-row-states). The field keeps its underline in `--sidebar-ring` and `outline-none` (`editable-name.tsx:147-148`).
  - Terminal row: the field is `TerminalNameField`, which replaces the button inside the same `SidebarMenuSubItem` (`terminal-rows.tsx:78-84`) and holds an `Input` with `data-slot="input"` (`terminal-rows.tsx:188-212`, `input.tsx:9`). The item also takes the input outline. The `Input` takes `focus-visible:ring-0`, which `cn` lets win over its `focus-visible:ring-3` (`input.tsx:11`; checked by running `cn` on 2026-11-11), so the row outline is the only focus mark; the input keeps its border and its `focus-visible:border-ring`.
  - Both fields take focus when they open (`editable-name.tsx:68-70`; `autoFocus`, `terminal-rows.tsx:195`). Selectors Level 4 §9.4 (`:focus-visible`, https://drafts.csswg.org/selectors-4/#the-focus-visible-pseudo, read 2026-11-11) gives user agents the heuristic "If the element supports keyboard input (such as an input element, or any other element that would triggers a virtual keyboard to be shown on focus if a physical keyboard were not present), indicate focus"; a text `input` and a `contentEditable` span are such elements. The heuristic is a "should", not a requirement, so the [1:1 verification](#11-verification) checks it in Electron for both fields and for the draft's field. Fallback, decided here: if Electron does not match `:focus-visible` on a field after it opens by the mouse, phase 5 keys the textbox and input outlines on `:focus` instead (`has-[[role=textbox]:focus]:outline-2 has-[[role=textbox]:focus]:-outline-offset-2 has-[[role=textbox]:focus]:outline-[var(--sidebar-ring)]`, and the same with `[data-slot=input]`; version 8 wrote them with `ring-2 ring-inset ring-sidebar-ring`), in every file that takes them, and T-5.5, T-5.11 and T-5.13 assert those tokens. `:focus` is enough there because these elements exist only while a name is being edited: the span has `role="textbox"` only while editing (`editable-name.tsx:135-140`), the draft's field only while the draft is open, and the `Input` only while the rename is open (`terminal-rows.tsx:78-84`). The treeitem, sub-button and menu-button outlines keep `:focus-visible`, so a mouse click on a row still draws no outline. The keyboard reveals keep `group-has-[:focus-visible]` ([§11.7](#117-reveals-on-keyboard-focus-only)), so under the fallback a Thread row being renamed shows its plus only while hovered. Where the heuristic holds, the row outline shows from the moment the rename opens, after a double-click or a context-menu Rename alike, until the field closes on Enter, Escape or blur (`editable-name.tsx:124-129`, `terminal-rows.tsx:198-211`).
  - While the Thread's field holds focus, the row's `treeitem` does not match `:focus-visible`, so the two rings never draw at once. Stop is not drawn while a terminal is being renamed (`terminal-rows.tsx:137`).
- Version 9. A 2 px outline offset by −2 px lies in the inner 2 px of the wrapper's border box (CSS Basic User Interface Level 4, `outline-offset`: the outline is drawn starting the offset away from the border edge), the band version 8's inset ring covered, and follows its 4 px corners (Chromium draws outlines along `border-radius`; Electron 44.4.3, `package.json:52`). Paint order differs from the ring's: a box shadow paints with the wrapper's background, under all its children, while the outline paints after the wrapper's in-flow content and under its positioned descendants (CSS 2.1 Appendix E). Neither order changes what shows, because every element that reaches the band is transparent there: the row button and the rename `Input`'s container have no background (the no-fill button; `terminal-rows.tsx:228` at `e63bbccb`), and the positioned elements below stay off the band or cross it as the guides do. In forced-colors mode the outline is still drawn in a system color, while the version 8 ring was a box shadow, which forced colors compute to `none` (CSS Color Adjustment Level 1, §3.1, https://drafts.csswg.org/css-color-adjust-1/, read 2026-11-11).
- Version 9, contrast (U17, SR19). The outline's adjacent colors are the wrapper's fill inside it and, outside it, `--sidebar` or a neighboring row's fill. `--sidebar-ring` has, light and dark: against `--sidebar` (an unselected row at rest) 3.40:1 and 10.19:1; against `--sidebar-accent` (a selected row) 3.16:1 and 9.19:1; against the hover fill 3.04:1 and 8.81:1; against the pressed fill 2.90:1 and 8.12:1. A row focused from the keyboard with no pointer on it is at rest or selected, so it meets WCAG 2.2 1.4.11's 3:1; only a focused row held pressed by the pointer drops to 2.90:1 in the light theme.
- The band's neighbors, as version 8 measured them for the ring, which covered the same band: the transparent button does not cover it. The mark backing (20 px tall, 4 px from the row's top and bottom, ending at the mark's 4 px right padding), the elapsed badge and the plus (both 20 px, 4 px from the top, `right-1`) stay 4 px inside the edges, so none covers the 2 px band. Stop (`size-6` from `size="icon-xs"`, `packages/desktop/src/components/button.tsx:31-32`; `top-0.5 right-1`, `terminal-rows.tsx:140-141`) is 24 px tall at 2 px from the 28 px row's top, so it ends 2 px above the bottom: its top and bottom edges lie flush against the band's inner edge and do not cover it, and its right edge is 4 px inside. Its hover square (`hover:bg-muted` in version 8, `hover:bg-[var(--paper-ghost-pressed)]` from version 9, D29; rounded) therefore meets the row outline along those edges without overlap; the [1:1 verification](#11-verification) checks it on a hovered, focused terminal row. The guide lines (`inset-y-0`) cross its top and bottom edges at each guide's x, 1 px wide, as accepted in SR19. Version 9: the terminal rename field has Stop's height, its `Input` `h-6` in an `h-7` container with `pr-1` (`terminal-rows.tsx:228`, `:234` at `e63bbccb`), so the input's border lies flush against the band's inner edge at the top and bottom and 4 px inside on the right, without overlap.
- Clipping. The nested Thread level is `SidebarMenuSub className={treeClassName}` (`thread-branches.tsx:212`), and `treeClassName` carries `overflow-hidden` with `mx-0 px-0` (`tree-guides.tsx:9-10`). Its rows are `w-full` list items holding a `w-full` wrapper (`thread-branches.tsx:136`, `:151`; terminal rows `terminal-rows.tsx:75`), so each wrapper lies whole inside the level's padding box: its fill, its 4 px corners and its row outline are not clipped. The button's outer ring of before phase 5 is clipped there at the row's left and right edges; the inset band is the reason nothing is clipped after phase 5. `treeClassName` does not change. The root level's scroller keeps `-mx-1.5 px-1.5` (`bundles/desktop/layout/src/client/components/organisms/project-sidebar.tsx:348-354`), which also keeps New Thread at x 6. In the Files column trees no ancestor between a row and the viewport clips ([§10.2](#102-rows), T-4.2); while a wide row is scrolled, the viewport cuts its left or right part, outline and corners included, like the rest of the row.
- Wide Files column trees (SR21). The tree is as wide as its widest row and every row is as wide as the tree ([§10.1](#101-the-scroll-container), [§10.2](#102-rows)). When the tree is wider than its viewport, every row wrapper is wider than the viewport, so a focused row's outline loses its right side at `scrollLeft` 0, its left side when scrolled to the end, and both in between; its top and bottom edges stay in view. Tab does not scroll sideways ([§10.3](#103-pinned-trailing-marks)), so the clipped side stays clipped. No mitigation in phase 5: an outline that follows the viewport would need an extra viewport-wide sticky element per row, the cost the user declined for the mark in version 4 ([Alternatives considered](#alternatives-considered)). Accepted by this spec; the user did not answer SQ6, so its default, no mitigation, holds.

#### 11.4 Terminal rows and row actions

Terminal rows (D23). A terminal row has no inner wrapper: its `SidebarMenuSubItem` (`group/terminal relative w-full`, `terminal-rows.tsx:73-76`) holds the guides, the button and Stop, and is the row wrapper. It takes `rounded-[4px]`, the unselected hover and pressed classes of [§11.1](#111-row-fill) and the focus classes of [§11.3](#113-keyboard-focus-and-clipping); its button takes the same no-fill classes as the other rows. Terminal rows have no mark. While the rename field is open, the item keeps these classes, the field draws over the fill, and the item draws the row outline while the field holds focus (D25, [§11.3](#113-keyboard-focus-and-clipping)). After phase 5 every row of the Thread tree fills the same way.

Selected terminal row (SQ4). The tab model is in Terms ("Tab, tab owner, selected tab"). Clicking a terminal row runs `opener` (`bundles/features/terminal/src/client/index.ts:116-135`): it navigates to the terminal's Thread when `layout.workspace/v1.thread()` knows it (`:129-133`), then opens, or only selects, the tab `terminal:<terminalId>` (`bundles/features/terminal/src/client/tab.ts:14-15`) among the active owner's tabs. When the Thread is not known, the route does not change and the tab opens under whichever Thread or Project the route names, so the selected tab can be a terminal of another Thread. A terminal row is therefore selected only when both hold: the route names the row's Thread (`NavigationV1.current()?.params.threadId`, `packages/bundle/src/contracts/basic/navigation.ts:12-18`, `:54`), and that Thread's selected tab is the terminal's tab. The terminal bundle already requires `layout.tabs/v1@^1.2.0` (`bundles/features/terminal/manifest.json`) and uses `NavigationV1` (`index.ts:15`, `:132`), so it needs no manifest change. The Files tree reads its selected file through `current()` the same way (`bundles/features/files/src/client/tree/files-tree.tsx:89`, `:104-108`).

- Data flow. The terminal rows element (`Rows`, `bundles/features/terminal/src/client/elements.tsx:84-104`, mounted once per expanded Thread with that Thread's id) reads the route's `threadId` from `NavigationV1` and the selected tab from `layout.tabs/v1`'s `current()`. It subscribes to `layout.tabs/v1` alone, since that service also notifies on every route change (Terms), and re-renders only when its answer changes. Its answer is the selected terminal id: the id inside the selected tab's id `terminal:<id>` (the form `terminalTabId` builds, `bundles/features/terminal/src/client/tab.ts:14-15`) when the route names this element's Thread and that id is not empty; otherwise none, including when either service is absent. The terminal bundle's mirror of `layout.tabs/v1` (`peers.ts:47-66`) gains `current` and `subscribe`, with the signatures the layout declares. The layout mounts the element under every expanded Thread, whether or not the Thread has terminals (`thread-branches.tsx:211-221` renders one `ElementHost` per registration, and the terminal registration is one of them). So `Rows` subscribes only while the store holds a terminal of its Thread: with none, it renders no row, holds no `layout.tabs/v1` subscription and answers none; when the first terminal of its Thread arrives, it subscribes, and when the last one leaves, it disposes the subscription. The plan decides the hooks and helpers.
- `TerminalRows` (props type `terminal-rows.tsx:36-56`) gains `readonly selectedId?: string`, documented as "the id of the terminal whose tab is the selected tab". A row is selected when `terminal.id === selectedId`: its item takes the selected fill and its button `isActive` (so `data-active`, which the no-fill button's `data-active:bg-transparent` keeps transparent) and `aria-current="true"`. Without `layout.tabs/v1` or `NavigationV1`, no row is selected.
- While a terminal row is selected, its Thread is the route's Thread, so that Thread's row is selected too: the parent and the terminal row under it both show the selected fill. The Files tree behaves alike (the Thread row and the selected file both show it). Accepted by the user with SQ4.
- Accessibility: terminal rows are buttons outside the `treeitem` pattern, as today, so `aria-selected` does not apply; the selected row's button carries `aria-current="true"`, which marks the current item of a set for assistive technology (WAI-ARIA 1.2, `aria-current`). `data-active` stays presentational. `SidebarMenuSubButton` passes the attribute through (`mergeProps`, `sidebar.tsx:695-703`).
- Cost (SR22). A `Rows` element is mounted under every expanded Thread, and only those whose Thread has terminals hold a `layout.tabs/v1` subscription (data flow, above); each holds one. That subscription fires on every change of a tab list, the route or the workspace tree (Terms), and each firing makes one `NavigationV1.current()` call and, on the Thread the route names, one `current()` call, which reads the owner's tab list (`layout-tabs.ts:136-147`). With E expanded Threads that have terminals and T tabs on the active owner, one firing costs E route reads plus one O(T) tab read, and re-renders at most the two `Rows` whose answer changed.

Row actions (version 9; line references in this part name the files at `e63bbccb`). In version 8 they kept their component styles. Version 9 gives them a `--paper-ghost-pressed` hover square and the action outline, 2 px of `--sidebar-ring` (D29, U17):

- The elapsed badge (`SidebarMenuBadge`, `row-add-action.tsx:53-63`) has no background (`sidebar.tsx:611`); it shows the row's fill. Unchanged.
- The plus (`SidebarMenuAction`, `row-add-action.tsx:70-78`) has the default `hover:bg-sidebar-accent` and `focus-visible:ring-2` in `ring-sidebar-ring` (`sidebar.tsx:586`). Its `className` adds the action hover set, `hover:bg-[var(--paper-ghost-pressed)]` (the class the `theme` variant gave it), and the action outline set. `SidebarMenuAction` joins its defaults and `className` with `cn` (`sidebar.tsx:584-590`), which drops `hover:bg-sidebar-accent` and keeps `outline-hidden`, `ring-sidebar-ring` and `focus-visible:ring-2` (run on 2026-11-11 on the default string and both sets).
- Remove and Retry (`SidebarMenuAction`, `screen/src/client/elements.tsx:190-202`, `:206-218`, and `mcp/src/client/elements.tsx:190-199`) have the same defaults. Each takes the action hover and action outline sets in its `className`, with the same result.
- Stop (`Button variant="ghost" size="icon-xs"`, `terminal-rows.tsx:181-184` at `e63bbccb`) has `hover:bg-muted` and `dark:hover:bg-muted/50` (`packages/desktop/src/components/button.tsx:19`); `--muted` equals `--sidebar-accent` in both themes (`tokens.css:15`, `:35`; `:110`, `:130`). Version 9: its `className` adds the Stop hover set, `hover:bg-[var(--paper-ghost-pressed)] dark:hover:bg-[var(--paper-ghost-pressed)]`. `Button` joins with `cn` (`button.tsx:54`), which drops both defaults (run on 2026-11-11). The `dark:` token is needed: `dark:` is `&:is(.dark *)` (`packages/desktop/src/styles/tailwind.css:11`), so `dark:hover:bg-muted/50` weighs one class more than a `hover:` rule and would win in a window with the `.dark` class, and `cn` drops it only for a token with the same variants. `aria-expanded:bg-muted` stays; Stop opens no popup. Its `className` also takes the action outline set and the Stop ring off set, `focus-visible:ring-0`: `cn` drops `focus-visible:ring-3` for `focus-visible:ring-0` and keeps `outline-none`, `focus-visible:ring-ring/50` (a ring color with no ring width left) and `focus-visible:border-ring` (run on 2026-11-11). Stop alone turns its ring off: `focus-visible:ring-3` is 3 px of `--ring` at 50% alpha (`button.tsx:6`), so under the 2 px outline it would show a 1 px band at half strength beyond it, while the other actions' ring is 2 px of `--sidebar-ring`, the outline's own width and color. Stop's classes come from the terminal bundle's sheet, which scans its source (`bundles/features/terminal/src/client/styles.css:7`), so the outline classes are in every window that has terminal 1.1.3. The 1 px border stays inside the outline in `--ring`, which equals `--sidebar-ring` in both themes (`tokens.css:25`, `:38`; `:120`, `:133`; `:196`, `:209`), so Stop's focus mark is 3 px of one color where the other actions' is 2 px.
- MCP's Sign in (`mcp/src/client/elements.tsx:177-188`) is a `<button>` written in mcp's source, with no background and no hover square, so it takes no hover set. Its class string (`:179`) keeps `ring-sidebar-ring`, `outline-hidden` and `focus-visible:ring-2` and adds the action outline set.
- The action outline set, `focus-visible:outline-solid focus-visible:outline-2 focus-visible:outline-[var(--sidebar-ring)]`. `focus-visible:outline-solid` is needed because each action carries `outline-hidden` (`sidebar.tsx:586`; Sign in, `mcp/src/client/elements.tsx:179`) or `outline-none` (`button.tsx:6`), and Tailwind 4.3.3 compiles both to `--tw-outline-style: none; outline-style: none`, while `outline-2` compiles to `outline-style: var(--tw-outline-style); outline-width: 2px` (compiled on 2026-11-11): without `outline-solid`, which sets `--tw-outline-style: solid` and `outline-style: solid`, the outline's style would resolve to `none` and draw nothing. The row wrappers carry neither class, so the row outline needs no such token. The outline has no offset token: it lies outside the action's border box, as the project list's button outline does (`project-item.tsx:84`), so the action's size and position do not change. In forced-colors mode `outline-hidden`'s own rule sets `outline-offset: 2px` on the plus, Remove, Retry and Sign in, moving their outline 2 px further out.
- The ring under the outline. The plus, Remove, Retry and Sign in keep `focus-visible:ring-2` in `ring-sidebar-ring`, a box shadow 2 px outside the border box that follows its corners, the band the outline covers and in its color; the outline paints over it (CSS 2.1 Appendix E), so they show one 2 px mark. In forced-colors mode the box shadow is not drawn and the outline is. They keep the ring for a window whose sheets hold the platform's defaults but not the outline classes: there `outline-hidden` holds, the outline draws nothing, and the ring, a `SidebarMenuAction` default in every renderer sheet (`sidebar.tsx:586`) or Sign in's own class, still marks focus. That window exists for the plus: [§11.5](#115-where-the-classes-live-and-mixed-versions), "Mixed versions", and SR16.
- Where the outline lies. The plus, Remove and Retry are 20 px squares 4 px from the row's top (`top-1` for the `sm` button, `sidebar.tsx:586`; MCP's Remove `top: 0.25rem`, `mcp/src/client/elements.tsx:193`) and at `right-1` or `right-7`; Sign in is 20 px tall at `top-1`; Stop is 24 px at `top-0.5 right-1`. At rest their outline therefore lies inside the 28 px row: 2 px or more inside it for the plus, Remove, Retry and Sign in (in forced-colors mode, with the 2 px offset, it reaches the row's top and bottom edges), and, for Stop, reaching the row's top and bottom edges and 2 px inside its right one. While Stop is pressed, `Button`'s `active:not-aria-[haspopup]:translate-y-px` (`button.tsx:6`) moves it 1 px down, outline included, so the outline's bottom edge lies 1 px below the row: over the next row's top edge, or, on the last row of a nested Thread level, cut off by `treeClassName`'s `overflow-hidden` (`packages/desktop/src/panels/tree-guides.tsx:10`). Stop shows its outline while pressed only when the press comes from the keyboard (Space held on a Stop focused by Tab) or the pointer presses a Stop that already matches `:focus-visible`. Accepted by this spec: the other three edges stay visible, and the shift lasts as long as the press. The action outline never meets the row outline: the row outline draws while a row control matches `:focus-visible`, and an action is not a row control (Terms, "Row control").

Why the squares take `--paper-ghost-pressed`. U17 gives the actions the rows' version 9 look; the preview's `theme` variant gave the plus this token, and D29 uses it for every square. Each square is a component default (`SidebarMenuAction`, `Button`'s `ghost` variant) that the call site overrides. Kept on the new fills, the default square (`--sidebar-accent`) would differ from a hovered row by 1.04:1 in both themes, in the direction opposite to the row's own hover: lighter than the row in the light theme (`#ece6db` on `#e7e2da`), while the row's hover darkens `--sidebar`, and darker in the dark theme (`#2d2924` on `#302c28`), while the row's hover lightens it. On a selected row it would equal the row (1:1), as in version 8. `--paper-ghost-pressed` darkens (light) or lightens (dark) whatever lies under it, the direction of the row's hover, so the square differs from its row by 1.16:1 to 1.27:1 in every state (the table below).

Why `packages/desktop/src/components/sidebar.tsx` is not in scope. Phase 5 put none of its row-state classes there (`git show --stat e63bbccb` lists no change to it); every set of [§11](#11-tree-row-states) is written at the call sites. The file is vendored from shadcn with recorded patches (`packages/desktop/PROVENANCE.md:108-110`), and the platform serves it to every bundle at run time, so a changed default would also change the rows of bundles built before phase 5. The same holds for `button.tsx`, whose `ghost` variant 64 call sites in `bundles/` and `packages/` use, Stop among them (counted on 2026-11-11, tests and stories excluded). The action sets go in the `className` of the six call sites: the plus, screen's Retry and Remove, mcp's Remove and Sign in, and Stop (and the story stand-in's Stop, [§11.5](#115-where-the-classes-live-and-mixed-versions)). That repeats the action outline set in six places of four bundles, the cost [§11](#11-tree-row-states) states for every set (D22, "What a token choice costs"); the shared default in `sidebar.tsx` would avoid it only by changing every older bundle's actions too.

The colors under the pointer, computed on 2026-11-11 from the tokens with sRGB alpha compositing, and the contrast ratio of each square against the row around it (WCAG 2.2 relative luminance). The plus, Remove, Retry and Stop take the same color. An action has no pressed color of its own: while the pointer is held down on it, it keeps its hover square and the row under it shows the pressed fill (SR18).

| Theme | Row hovered | Action hovered on a hovered row | Pointer held down on the action (row pressed) | Action hovered on a selected row |
| ----- | ----------- | ------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------- |
| Light | `#e7e2da`   | `#d7d2ca`, 1.17:1               | `#d2cec5` on `#e2ddd4`, 1.16:1                | `#dcd6cb` on `#ece6db`, 1.16:1   |
| Dark  | `#302c28`   | `#403c37`, 1.27:1               | `#44413c` on `#35322d`, 1.26:1                | `#3d3933` on `#2d2924`, 1.26:1   |

An action's hover square shows on a selected row in both themes. The [1:1 verification](#11-verification) checks this table.

The action outline lies just outside the action. Its outer neighbor is the row's fill, so its contrast there is the row outline's ([§11.3](#113-keyboard-focus-and-clipping)): light and dark, 3.40:1 and 10.19:1 on an unselected row at rest, 3.16:1 and 9.19:1 on a selected row, 3.04:1 and 8.81:1 on a hovered row, 2.90:1 and 8.12:1 on a pressed row. Its inner neighbor is the action's own background: transparent, so the same row fill, except while the pointer is on the action, when it is the hover square: in the light theme 2.61:1 on a hovered row's square, 2.71:1 on a selected row's and 2.50:1 while pressed (dark 6.96:1, 7.29:1, 6.46:1). An action reached by Tab with no pointer on it sits on a row at rest or selected, at 3:1 or more on both sides. Why the outer form and not the rows' inset one: the inset outline would have the same neighbors, and on the 20 px plus, Remove and Retry it would cover the square's outer 2 px up to the edge of their 16 px icon (`[&>svg]:size-4`, `sidebar.tsx:586`); the outer form keeps the square, the icon and the position of version 8's ring as they are.

#### 11.5 Where the classes live and mixed versions

No new export (D22). The class sets of [§11](#11-tree-row-states) are written as complete string literals in a source file a sheet scans, because Tailwind's scanner reads whole class tokens from the source (`app/desktop/doric-renderer/src/styles.css:15-19`; each bundle sheet, for example `bundles/features/git/src/client/styles.css:10-23`); screen and mcp also list theirs in their sheets ([§11.6](#116-pane-rows-and-the-draft-row)). Within one package or bundle, the files that draw rows may share one copy of the sets, for example layout's `thread-branches.tsx` and `draft-name.tsx` through a module such as `domain/sidebar.ts`, which already holds `threadRowPadding`. No set crosses a bundle boundary, and the platform exports nothing new: `packages/desktop/src/panels/index.ts` re-exports every export of `file-tree.tsx` and `tree-guides.tsx` (`export *`), so a shared copy in the platform stays in a module that `index.ts` does not re-export. The plan decides where each package or bundle keeps its sets (constant or function, module and names) and how each element joins them with its other classes. The components already pass `className` through `cn` (`sidebar.tsx:497`, `:536`, `:679`, `:700-703`; `input.tsx:10-13`), so the sets override the defaults whichever join is used ([§11.1](#111-row-fill)).

Which element takes which sets, with lines at `7e0237ba`. Each element keeps its other classes except those named as dropped; "by selection" means the selected set on a selected row and the unselected set otherwise ([§11.1](#111-row-fill)):

Version 9 keeps every assignment of the table above. It rewrites the tokens of the unselected fill, the row outlines and the unselected mark backing where phase 5 wrote them, and adds the action hover, Stop hover, action outline and Stop ring off sets where the table says "version 9". [Files to change](#phase-5-amendment-version-9) gives, per file, the lines at `e63bbccb` where phase 5 wrote the rewritten tokens and where the new sets go; the plan decides names and sharing (D22).

The no-fill button set, the selected sets and the keyboard reveals are not rewritten. The doc comments of the rewritten sets that name "the accent mixed in at 50% … 80%" (`file-tree.tsx:24-29`, `sidebar.ts:26-32`, `changes-tree.tsx:32-36`, `terminal-rows.tsx:34-38`, `screen/…/elements.tsx:136-140`, `mcp/…/elements.tsx:114-118` at `e63bbccb`) and "inset ring" are reworded to the paper tokens and the outline.

`packages/desktop/src/panels/tree-guides.tsx`: phase 5 kept `rowInteraction`'s signature and value and gave it a `@deprecated` doc comment (`tree-guides.tsx:20` at `e63bbccb`): it is kept for bundles built before phase 5, which call it on newer apps, its value must not change, and it goes only in a platform major. Version 9 does not change the file.

Versions. Bundle versions follow their largest change (`docs/architecture/bundles/versioning.md:57`): markup only, no contract change, so patches: layout 3.0.1 → 3.0.2, git 1.2.2 → 1.2.3, terminal 1.1.2 → 1.1.3, screen 2.0.2 → 2.0.3, mcp 1.2.3 → 1.2.4. The terminal bundle's new calls to `layout.tabs/v1.current` and `subscribe` sit inside its existing `requires` range (`layout.tabs/v1@^1.2.0`), and a bundle sheet is packaging, not a contract or contribution, so neither raises a minor. The files bundle does not change: its tree is the platform's `FileTree` (`bundles/features/files/src/client/tree/files-tree.tsx`), so it stays 1.7.1. `PLATFORM_VERSION` stays 1.6.0: it rises a minor per phase that adds to the platform (`versioning.md:31`, `packages/bundle/src/runtime/version.ts:1-10`), and phase 5 adds nothing; phase 1 changed `tree-guides.tsx`'s step the same way without a platform minor ([Contracts and versions](#contracts-and-versions)). No `engines.doric` changes.

Versions, version 9. The amendment moves no version. Phase 5 is unreleased: `e63bbccb` is on `origin/feat/sidebar-alignment` only and no tag contains it (`git tag --contains e63bbccb` is empty, 2026-11-11), and the manifests there already carry the phase 5 versions (`"version"` at line 4 of each: layout 3.0.2, git 1.2.3, terminal 1.1.3, screen 2.0.3, mcp 1.2.4, files 1.7.1; `PLATFORM_VERSION = '1.6.0'`, `packages/bundle/src/runtime/version.ts:10`). The amendment changes the markup and the sheets of those unreleased versions in the same pull request, so no build of them with the version 8 classes leaves the branch.

Mixed versions. The app and the bundles are not released together; bundles may be newer or older than the app (`versioning.md:33`; UR25, `docs/designs/ui-redesign.md:103`, `:196`).

- Newer bundles on an app built before phase 5. Layout, git and terminal import no name the app lacks (they drop `rowInteraction` and add nothing), so they link and activate. Thread, terminal and repository rows draw the new states. The app's `FileTree` keeps today's look in the Files view and under the repository row: no unselected hover fill, a selected fill that ends at the mark, the `bg-sidebar/90` mark backing. The layout bundle ships no utilities sheet of its own (`bundles/desktop/layout/src/client/styles.css:7-9`), and the older app's renderer sheet does not hold the new classes; the files, git and terminal sheets scan the layout's client source at their build (`bundles/features/files/src/client/styles.css:18`, `bundles/features/git/src/client/styles.css:21`, `bundles/features/terminal/src/client/styles.css:33`). Git 1.2.3 and terminal 1.1.3 are built with phase 5, so their sheets hold the layout's new classes and bring them to the window. The files bundle keeps 1.7.1: its sheet holds them only when that bundle was built from a tree that includes phase 5 (the bundles of one host are built together, `docs/designs/ui-redesign.md:196`), and a files 1.7.1 built before does not, so this spec counts only the git and terminal sheets as carriers. The terminal's own classes, the input outline included, come from its sheet, which scans its own source (`bundles/features/terminal/src/client/styles.css:7`). SR16.
- Screens and MCP. Neither bundle ships a utility sheet today (`src/client/` of each holds no `styles.css`), and no other sheet scans their source: their classes reach the window only through the app's renderer sheet (`app/desktop/doric-renderer/src/styles.css:19`). A newer screen or mcp on an older app would find neither the fill classes nor the `has-[…]` outline classes there, while the button's `focus-visible:ring-0`, already used elsewhere and so in the older sheet, would still remove the button's own ring: the row would lose every keyboard focus mark. So phase 5 adds the minimal bundle sheets `bundles/features/screen/src/client/styles.css` and `bundles/features/mcp/src/client/styles.css` (U15), which list the classes phase 5 adds to each bundle and which the bundle build picks up by that path (`packages/bundle/src/build/parts.ts:321`) and the loader serves as `clientStyles` (`packages/bundle/src/loader/distribution.ts:147`) ([§11.6](#116-pane-rows-and-the-draft-row), SR20). Old screen 2.0.2 and mcp 1.2.3 on a newer app keep their look: hovering the row hovers its full-width button, whose default `hover:bg-sidebar-accent` is in the platform's source (`sidebar.tsx:497`) and so in every sheet.
- Version 9, the tokens on an older app. The classes read `--paper-ghost-hover`, `--paper-ghost-pressed`, `--sidebar-ring` and `--sidebar`, which an app defines in its `theme.css` (Terms, "Renderer sheet, bundle sheet"). All four entered `packages/desktop/src/tokens/tokens.ts` in `96cbe110` (2026-10-18, PR #21), the commit that also introduced `PLATFORM_VERSION` (`git log -S` on both files), so every app that a bundle with `engines.doric` `^1.0.0` can load defines them. An undefined ghost token would have made the fill transparent and dropped the mark backing's image layer, leaving `bg-sidebar`; that case does not arise.
- Version 9, the plus on an app built before phase 5 with neither git 1.2.3 nor terminal 1.1.3 active (SR16). The layout's new classes have no rule in that window, so the plus keeps what the renderer sheet holds for it: its `SidebarMenuAction` defaults (`sidebar.tsx:586`), of which `cn` has dropped only `hover:bg-sidebar-accent`. Under Tab it shows its version 8 ring, 2 px of `--sidebar-ring` outside the square: `focus-visible:ring-2` and `ring-sidebar-ring` have rules there, while `focus-visible:outline-solid` and `focus-visible:outline-[var(--sidebar-ring)]` do not (no source writes them at `bb4c2453` or at `e63bbccb`), and `focus-visible:outline-2`, which does (`project-item.tsx:83` at `bb4c2453`), resolves to `outline-style: none` under `outline-hidden` ([§11.4](#114-terminal-rows-and-row-actions)). Under the pointer it shows no square: `hover:bg-[var(--paper-ghost-pressed)]` has no rule, and the row draws no hover fill either. The exception is a window that also runs screen 2.0.3 or mcp 1.2.4: their sheets list `hover:bg-[var(--paper-ghost-pressed)]` and the three outline tokens ([§11.6](#116-pane-rows-and-the-draft-row)), and a listed rule applies to every element of the window that carries the class, the plus included, which then shows its square and its outline over its ring. The first draft of D29 also gave the plus `focus-visible:ring-0`, which that sheet does hold (`bundles/features/git/src/client/cwd/branch-picker.tsx:61`, `bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/components/molecules/model-picker.tsx:102`, `packages/desktop/src/components/input-group.tsx:125`, `:141` at `bb4c2453`): the plus would have lost its ring and drawn no outline, no focus mark at all. Remove, Retry, Sign in and Stop are not affected: their bundles' sheets carry every class version 9 gives them ([§11.6](#116-pane-rows-and-the-draft-row); `bundles/features/terminal/src/client/styles.css:7`).
- Older bundles on an app with phase 5. Layout 3.0.1 and git 1.2.2 call the unchanged `rowInteraction` and draw today's Thread and repository rows; terminal 1.1.2 keeps its full-accent hover; the app's `FileTree` draws the new states, its classes emitted by the app's renderer sheet (`app/desktop/doric-renderer/src/styles.css:18`). Nothing fails, and every selected row keeps its fill.

#### 11.6 Pane rows and the draft row

D24 (SQ3, U15).

Screens and MCP rows. The row wrapper is the `SidebarMenuItem` (`group/menu-item relative`, `packages/desktop/src/components/sidebar.tsx:485-490`; `bundles/features/screen/src/client/elements.tsx:161`, `bundles/features/mcp/src/client/elements.tsx:145`); its button is a `SidebarMenuButton`, a `<button>` with `data-slot="sidebar-menu-button"` (the slot the pane tests already query, `bundles/features/screen/tests/client/sidebar.test.ts:313`).

- Neither pane has a selection: a Screens row opens or focuses its view's window (`screen/src/client/elements.tsx:4-5`, `:167-169`) and an MCP row opens the server's edit dialog (`mcp/src/client/elements.tsx:150`). So no pane row has a selected fill.
- The `SidebarMenuItem` takes the unselected fill and the menu-button outline of [§11](#11-tree-row-states). A Screens row's button is disabled while the screens service is absent (`const absent = screens === undefined`, `screen/src/client/elements.tsx:158`; `disabled={absent}`, `:165`); such a row's `SidebarMenuItem` takes none of these classes, in version 8 or 9: its button takes no focus and does not react to the pointer, so neither a fill nor an outline would show, and a corner radius alone draws nothing. Its Retry and Remove are `disabled` too (`screen/src/client/elements.tsx:196`, `:212` at `e63bbccb`) and keep the classes they take on an enabled row, as in version 8 they kept their defaults; a disabled `<button>` takes no focus, so their action outline never draws.
- The button (`screen/src/client/elements.tsx:164`, `mcp/src/client/elements.tsx:148`) drops `group-focus-within/menu-item:bg-sidebar-accent` and `group-hover/menu-item:bg-sidebar-accent`, keeps its other classes, and takes the no-fill button set; `SidebarMenuButton` merges it with `cn` (`sidebar.tsx:536`).
- Focus no longer fills the row. Today any focus inside the row fills it in `--sidebar-accent`, including the focus a mouse click leaves on the button; after phase 5 keyboard focus on the button draws the row outline, and focus on a row action draws that action's own focus mark, from version 9 the action outline.
- The overlaid actions: Remove and Retry (`SidebarMenuAction`, `screen/src/client/elements.tsx:177-206`; `mcp/src/client/elements.tsx:177-186`) kept `hover:bg-sidebar-accent` (`sidebar.tsx:586`) in version 8; version 9 gives them the action hover set, `hover:bg-[var(--paper-ghost-pressed)]`, and the action outline set (D29), whose colors the table of [§11.4](#114-terminal-rows-and-row-actions) gives. What reveals them, and the status fade (`screen/…/elements.tsx:173`, `mcp/…/elements.tsx:155`), moves to keyboard focus ([§11.7](#117-reveals-on-keyboard-focus-only)). MCP's "Sign in" link (`mcp/src/client/elements.tsx:164-174`) has no background; version 9 adds the action outline set over its own `focus-visible:ring-2` (D29); pressing it, like pressing an action, shows the row's pressed fill (SR18).
- Clipping: the rows are `w-full` items of the pane's `SidebarMenu` (`screen/src/client/elements.tsx:234`), inside the pane content that scrolls (`overflow-y-auto`, T-3.4); the wrapper lies whole inside it, so the fill, corners and row outline are not clipped.

Minimal bundle sheets (U15). `bundles/features/screen/src/client/styles.css` and `bundles/features/mcp/src/client/styles.css` are new.

- The rule they depart from. Every utility sheet in the repository emits the whole sorted set of utilities the window uses, so that a class two sheets emit keeps the order one compiled sheet gives it: the renderer sheet (`app/desktop/doric-renderer/src/styles.css:9-13`); the files, git and terminal sheets, which also scan the layout, chat and settings sources since WP-10N (`bundles/features/git/src/client/styles.css:11-23`, `docs/architecture/bundles/implementation-decisions.md:991`); the chat sheet (`bundles/desktop/chat/src/client/styles.css:18-24`); and the layout bundle, which emits no utilities for that reason (`bundles/desktop/layout/src/client/styles.css:7-9`; WP6, `implementation-decisions.md:804`: a later sheet that re-emits a subset moves those classes in the cascade relative to the single renderer sheet). The two sheets here re-emit a subset. U15 accepts that departure; the list and the check below bound it.
- Content. Each sheet holds exactly a first comment and three lines:

  ```css
  @import 'tailwindcss/utilities.css' layer(utilities) source(none);
  @reference 'desktop/ui/tailwind.css';
  @source inline('…');
  ```

  The comment reads "The screen bundle's phase 5 utilities only, a subset of the window's sorted set (docs/designs/sidebar-alignment.md §11.6). Run that section's cascade check before adding a class.", and the same for mcp. The `inline` string is the bundle's list, its tokens separated by single spaces in the order given here:
  - screen, 18 tokens (version 9): `rounded-[4px]`, `hover:bg-[var(--paper-ghost-hover)]`, `active:bg-[var(--paper-ghost-pressed)]`, `has-[[data-slot=sidebar-menu-button]:focus-visible]:outline-2`, `has-[[data-slot=sidebar-menu-button]:focus-visible]:-outline-offset-2`, `has-[[data-slot=sidebar-menu-button]:focus-visible]:outline-[var(--sidebar-ring)]`, `bg-transparent`, `hover:bg-transparent`, `active:bg-transparent`, `data-active:bg-transparent`, `focus-visible:ring-0`, `hover:bg-[var(--paper-ghost-pressed)]`, `focus-visible:outline-solid`, `focus-visible:outline-2`, `focus-visible:outline-[var(--sidebar-ring)]`, `group-has-[:focus-visible]/menu-item:pointer-events-auto`, `group-has-[:focus-visible]/menu-item:opacity-100`, `group-has-[:focus-visible]/menu-item:opacity-0`;
  - mcp, 19 tokens (version 9): the screen list and `group-has-[:focus-visible]/menu-item:right-7`. Sign in's action outline set adds no token beyond the list, since Remove carries the same three.

  These lists replace the ones `e63bbccb` ships (`bundles/features/screen/src/client/styles.css:8`, `bundles/features/mcp/src/client/styles.css:8`). `focus-visible:ring-0` is the no-fill button's; no screen or mcp action carries it (D29). The action outline set needs no `outline-offset` token: its outline is outer ([§11.4](#114-terminal-rows-and-row-actions)).

  These are the classes phase 5 adds to the two bundles (the element table of [§11.5](#115-where-the-classes-live-and-mixed-versions)), the ones an app built before phase 5 may lack. Every other class of the two bundles has a rule in the renderer sheet of any app built with their sources, as before phase 5.

- Why a list and not a scan. Version 8 first had each sheet scan its bundle's `src/client/` (`@source './'`, about 8 KB and 15 KB). The cascade check below, run on those sheets on 2026-11-11, found re-emitted classes that change elements of other bundles, in every window, since every window loads every bundle sheet (Terms, "Renderer sheet, bundle sheet"):
  - mcp's `border` after `border-l-3` turns the 3 px left edge of a comment line into 1 px (`bundles/desktop/chat/src/client/components/molecules/comment-line.tsx:134`, `bundles/desktop/chat/src/client/components/organisms/comment-entry.tsx:445`);
  - mcp's `transition-transform` after `motion-reduce:transition-none` animates the thread rail's icon under reduced motion (`bundles/desktop/chat/src/client/components/molecules/thread-nav-rail.tsx:206`; removed since, the rail no longer draws that icon);
  - mcp's `hover:text-foreground` after `dark:text-muted-foreground` changes a hovered inactive tab's text in the dark theme (`packages/desktop/src/components/tabs.tsx:58`).

  The scanned sheets gave 28 (screen) and 73 (mcp) candidate pairs found on one element or through a `*:` rule (step 3 of the check), each to be judged by hand on every change to either bundle. The lists hold only the phase 5 classes, and their check below finds no conflict. The user kept the lists (SQ9, closed).

- Build. `source(none)` turns off Tailwind's automatic scan, as the renderer sheet does (`app/desktop/doric-renderer/src/styles.css:15`), and `@source inline(…)` makes the listed tokens candidates without reading a file (Tailwind 4.3.3). The bundle build compiles the sheet like any bundle sheet (`packages/bundle/src/build/parts.ts:253-281`). Compiled on 2026-11-11 through those steps: about 4.8 KB each, unminified, most of it the `@property` declarations of the ring and shadow variables (the version 8 lists; `bundles/features/screen/dist/client/styles.css` is 4 670 bytes and the mcp one 4 832 at `e63bbccb`). Version 9: the version 9 lists compiled in memory with `@tailwindcss/node` 4.3.3 against the same `@reference` give 4 604 bytes (screen) and 4 766 bytes (mcp) before the bundle build's own additions, and emit `background-color: var(--paper-ghost-hover)`, `outline-color: var(--sidebar-ring)` and `outline-offset: calc(2px * -1)` under `:has(:is([data-slot=sidebar-menu-button]:focus-visible))`, the action outline rules under `:focus-visible` (`outline-style: solid`, `outline-width: 2px`, `outline-color: var(--sidebar-ring)`), and `--tw-outline-style: solid` among the `@property` fallbacks that `outline-2` needs (`outline-style: var(--tw-outline-style)`). Both stay under T-5.12's 8 192-byte bound. The files bundle's full sheet is 163 KB (`bundles/features/files/dist/client/styles.css`).
- Cascade constraint. The sheet loads after the renderer sheet and after the bundle sheets activated before it (Terms, "Renderer sheet, bundle sheet"), so each rule R it emits comes after every rule of those sheets. On an element that matches R and a rule Q of an earlier sheet, the new order changes the result when all four hold:
  - Q sets a property R sets; a shorthand counts as each of its longhands (`border` and `border-l-3`; `p-2` and `px-1`).
  - Q has R's specificity.
  - Q comes after R in the one sorted set, the renderer sheet, so on a single sheet Q wins.
  - The sheet does not also emit Q.

  Specificity is computed from each compiled selector (Selectors Level 4 §17: `:where()` adds nothing, `:is()` and `:has()` add their most specific argument, an attribute selector or pseudo-class adds as much as a class). What each variant adds in Tailwind 4.3.3 with this repository's variants, read from the compiled renderer sheet on 2026-11-11:
  - One class's worth:
    - a pseudo-class or `:has()`: `hover:`, `active:`, `focus-visible:`, `disabled:`, `has-[…]:`, `group-hover/…:`, `group-has-[…]/…:`;
    - an attribute selector: Tailwind's own `aria-*:` (`.aria-invalid\:border-destructive[aria-invalid="true"]`) and bracketed `data-[…]:` (`.data-\[side\=bottom\]\:inset-x-0[data-side="bottom"]`), `has-data-[…]:` (`.has-data-\[icon\=inline-end\]\:pr-1:has([data-icon="inline-end"])`), `group-data-[…]/…:` (`:is(:where(.group)[data-collapsible="icon"] *)`), `group-has-data-[…]/…:` and `peer-data-[…]/…:`;
    - `dark:`, which is `&:is(.dark *)` (`packages/desktop/src/styles/tailwind.css:11`).
  - Nothing:
    - media, container and `supports-*` variants (`md:`, `@md/field-group:`, `motion-reduce:`);
    - the named data variants shadcn defines, `data-open`, `data-closed`, `data-checked`, `data-unchecked`, `data-selected`, `data-disabled`, `data-active`, `data-horizontal` and `data-vertical`, each a `:where()` (`node_modules/shadcn/dist/tailwind.css:28-86`; `.data-active\:bg-transparent:where([data-state="active"]), …`), and their `group-*` forms (`group-data-open/x:`);
    - `in-[…]:` and `in-data-[…]:` (`:where([data-side="left"]) .in-data-\[side\=left\]\:cursor-w-resize`), `ltr:` and `rtl:` (`:where(:dir(ltr), …)`);
    - the child and descendant variants `*:` and `**:` (`:is(.\*\:w-full > *)`, one class). They sit on the parent and match its children, the case the chat sheet names: a field's `*:w-full` over a picker trigger's `w-fit` (`bundles/desktop/chat/src/client/styles.css:19-22`, `packages/desktop/src/components/field.tsx:58`, `packages/desktop/src/components/select.tsx:42`). The platform also uses `*:[a]:underline`, `*:data-[slot=…]:…` and `**:data-[slot=kbd]:…` (`dialog.tsx:143`, `combobox.tsx:114`, `tooltip.tsx:52`).

  Two rules with the same variant and the same property set (`px-1` and `px-2`) are alternatives that `cn` never leaves on one element (`packages/desktop/package.json:138`), so they are not a pair.

- The cascade check. Run it on any change to either list:
  1. Build the renderer sheet and the bundle sheet. Read each rule's selector, its conditions (`@media`, `@container`, `@supports`) and its properties after nesting is resolved.
  2. List every pair (R, Q) the constraint names.
  3. For each pair, look for an element that matches both. For most variants that is one element: classes from one `cn` call, one `cva`, one `className`, or a component's defaults joined with its caller's `className`. For a `*:` or `**:` rule Q, it is a parent that carries Q and a child that carries R. The elements include those phase 5 changes, with the classes it leaves them (today's classes, minus those the element table of [§11.5](#115-where-the-classes-live-and-mixed-versions) drops, plus the sets it adds, joined through `cn`): before phase 5 is written the source does not hold those classes, so a search of the source alone misses them.
  4. A pair on a real element whose result changes is a conflict. The class then leaves the list, so its row relies on the renderer sheet, or the element changes.

  Result on 2026-11-11 at `7e0237ba`, with the lists above and the specificity of each compiled selector: step 3 leaves ten pairs per sheet, and none is a conflict. (The first run of this check, in the stage 3 amendment, searched today's source only and stated the specificity rule wrongly; it reported seven pairs and missed the two `data-open` pairs and the `aria-expanded` pair below.)
  - `hover:bg-[…50%…]`, `active:bg-[…80%…]`, `hover:bg-transparent` and `active:bg-transparent` against `*:data-[slot=input-group]:bg-input/30`; `focus-visible:ring-0` against `*:data-[slot=input-group]:shadow-none` (`packages/desktop/src/components/combobox.tsx:114`). The combobox's input group carries none of these classes (`input-group.tsx:15`, `combobox.tsx:63`). Also, `ring-0` and `shadow-none` both write `box-shadow` from the same `--tw-*` variables, so their order does not change it.
  - `focus-visible:ring-0` against `aria-invalid:ring-0` (`input-group.tsx:125`, `:141`): the same value.
  - `focus-visible:ring-0` against `aria-invalid:ring-3`, on the branch and model search inputs (`bundles/features/git/src/client/cwd/branch-picker.tsx:61`, `bundles/desktop/settings/src/client/components/molecules/model-picker.tsx:102`) and, after phase 5, the terminal rename `Input` (`terminal-rows.tsx:188-212`): none sets `aria-invalid`.
  - `hover:bg-transparent` and `active:bg-transparent` against `data-open:hover:bg-sidebar-accent` (`sidebar.tsx:497`), on every `SidebarMenuButton` that takes the no-fill button: the Thread, `FileTree`, repository, Screens and MCP buttons. `data-open` matches `[data-state="open"]` or a `data-open` attribute (`node_modules/shadcn/dist/tailwind.css:28-33`). base-ui triggers mark an open popup with `data-popup-open` and a press with `data-pressed` (`node_modules/@base-ui/react/utils/CommonTriggerDataAttributes.js:10`, `:15`), and the repository's own code writes `data-state` only on the sidebar container (`sidebar.tsx:230`) and a comment pin (`packages/desktop/src/comment-pin.tsx:70`) and writes no `data-open`. No row button is a popup, so none matches Q.
  - `group-has-[:focus-visible]/menu-item:opacity-100` against `aria-expanded:opacity-100` (`sidebar.tsx:588`): `SidebarMenuAction` adds Q only with `showOnHover`, which no caller passes, and both set the same value.

  Result on 2026-11-11 at `e63bbccb` for the version 9 lists, run the same way on the renderer sheet compiled in memory from `app/desktop/doric-renderer/src/styles.css` with the version 9 tokens added, its rules read with `postcss` and each selector's specificity computed per Selectors Level 4 §17: step 3 leaves thirteen pairs per sheet, and none is a conflict.
  - The ten pairs above, with `hover:bg-[var(--paper-ghost-hover)]` and `active:bg-[var(--paper-ghost-pressed)]` in place of the 50% and 80% tokens. The two fill tokens set the same property (`background-color`) under the same variants as the mixes did, so they meet the same Q, and the judgment given for each pair above holds.
  - `hover:bg-[var(--paper-ghost-pressed)]` against `*:data-[slot=input-group]:bg-input/30`, as in the first pair above: no combobox input group carries it.
  - `hover:bg-[var(--paper-ghost-pressed)]` against `aria-expanded:bg-muted` (`button.tsx:19`, the `ghost` variant), on Stop, which the re-emitted rule also reaches: Stop never carries `aria-expanded`, since it opens no popup and `Button` sets none (`terminal-rows.tsx:181-191` at `e63bbccb`), so Q never applies to it.
